P^{\mathrm{std}}=P_{n}\times P^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}
, which is a standard parabolic subgroup of
G
. This is the
standardization
of
P
. Write
(n_{i})=\underline{n}(P^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1})
. We may decompose the standard Levi factor
M_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}
of
P^{\mathrm{std}}
as

M_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}=M_{P,n}^{\mathrm{std}}\times M_{P,n+1}^{\mathrm{std}}=\left(\mathbf{M}_{P,+}\times\mathcal{M}_{P,n}\times\mathbf{M}_{P,-}\right)\times\left(\mathbf{M}_{P,+}\times\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{std}}_{P,n+1}\times\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{std}}_{P,-}\right),

(4.6)

where

\mathbf{M}_{P,+}=\prod_{i<i_{P}}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}},\quad\mathbf{M}_{P,-}\simeq\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{std}}_{P,-}=\prod_{i>i_{P}}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}},\quad\mathcal{M}_{P,n}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{P}}-1},\quad\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{std}}_{P,n+1}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{P}}}.

(4.7)

We add a
std
on
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{std}}_{P,-}
to emphasize that, although they are isomorphic, the groups
\mathbf{M}_{P,-}
and
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{std}}_{P,-}
are not identified by the embedding (
4.1
). By composing with
w_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}
, we get a decomposition

M_{P}\simeq\left(\mathbf{M}_{P,+}\times\mathcal{M}_{P,n}\times\mathbf{M}_{P,-}\right)\times\left(\mathbf{M}_{P,+}\times\mathcal{M}_{P,n+1}\times\mathbf{M}_{P,-}\right).

(4.8)

The two copies of
\mathbf{M}_{P,-}
in (
4.8
) are now identified by the embedding (
4.1
). The groups
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{std}}_{P,n+1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{P,n+1}
are isomorphic but in general embedded in two different ways in
\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}
. Set

\mathbf{M}_{P}=\mathbf{M}_{P,+}\times\mathbf{M}_{P,-},\quad\mathbf{M}_{P}^{2}=\mathbf{M}_{P}\times\mathbf{M}_{P},\quad\mathcal{M}_{P}=\mathcal{M}_{P,n}\times\mathcal{M}_{P,n+1}.

The restriction of the diagonal embedding
H\subset G
gives
\mathcal{M}_{P,H}\subset\mathcal{M}_{P}
and we have
\mathcal{M}_{P,H}\simeq\mathcal{M}_{P,n}
. Note that
M_{P_{H}}=M_{P,H}=\mathbf{M}_{P}\times\mathcal{M}_{P,H}
. The group
\mathcal{M}_{P}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{P}}-1}\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{P}}}
, and we can define embedding of
\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{P}}-1}
in
\mathcal{M}_{P}
as in (
4.1
). This is compatible with the inclusion
H\subset G
in the sense that
\mathcal{M}_{P,H}=\mathcal{M}_{P}\cap\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{P}}-1}
. In particular, we have
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{M}_{P}}_{\RS}
the set of Rankin–Selberg parabolic subgroups of
\mathcal{M}_{P}
. We describe the relative version of the set
\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. We have a bijection

P\in\left\{P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\;|\;P\subset Q\right\}\mapsto(P\cap\mathbf{M}_{Q},P\cap\mathcal{M}_{Q})\in\{\text{standard parabolic subgroups of }\mathbf{M}_{Q}\}\times\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q}}_{\RS}.

(4.9)

Moreover, for any
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
with
P\subset Q
we have

\mathcal{M}_{P}\subset\mathcal{M}_{Q},\quad w_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}=w_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q},\mathrm{std}}w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}},

where
w_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q},\mathrm{std}}
is
w_{P\cap\mathcal{M}_{Q}}^{\mathrm{std}}
seen as an element in
G
via the embedding
\mathcal{M}_{Q}\subset G
.

Proof.

This follows from the classification of Corollary
4.2
, noting that if
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
with
P\subset Q
, then
P_{H}\subset Q_{H}
and therefore
\underline{n}(P_{H})
is a subdivision of
\underline{n}(Q_{H})
.
∎

4.1.4
Connection to
[
58
]

We now connect the Rankin–Selberg parabolic subgroups to the constructions of
[
58
, Sections 3.1 and 3.2]
. For any semi-standard parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
, set

\mathfrak{z}_{P}=\mathfrak{a}_{P}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{0,H},\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}=\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{+}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}.

If
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
, write
M_{P,n+1}^{\mathrm{std}}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}
. We have the descriptions

\mathfrak{z}_{P}=\left\{H=(H_{n},H_{n+1})\in\mathfrak{a}_{P}\;\Big|\;\begin{array}[]{l}H_{n,i}=H_{n+1,i}\text{ if }i\neq i_{P},\\
H_{n,i_{P}}=H_{n+1,i_{P}}=0\end{array}\right\}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathbf{M}_{P}}=\oplus_{i\neq i_{P}}\mathfrak{a}_{\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}},

(4.10)

and

\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}=\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{+}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{P_{H}}^{+}=\left\{H\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}\;|\;H_{1}/n_{1}>\ldots>H_{i_{P}-1}/{n_{i_{P}-1}}>0>H_{i_{P}+1}/{n_{i_{P}+1}}>\ldots>H_{m}/n_{m}\right\}

(4.11)

where we write
H_{i}
for
H_{n,i}=H_{n+1,i}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
P_{H}
be a standard parabolic subgroup of
H
. We have

\mathcal{P}_{\RS}(P_{H})=\left\{Q\subset G\;\text{semi-standard}\;|\;\mathfrak{a}_{Q}^{+}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{P_{H}}^{+}\neq\emptyset\right\}.

(4.12)

Remark 4.5
.

The set in the RHS of (
4.12
) is defined in
[
58
, 23]
by asking that
Q
the split center of the centralizer of
T_{0,H}
in
G
instead of
T_{0}
. As
Z_{\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}}T_{0,n}=T_{0,n+1}
, this is
T_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
Q
belong to the RHS of (
4.12
). By
[
58
, Proposition 3.1]
, we have
Q_{H}=P_{H}
,
M_{Q,H}=M_{P,H}
and
N_{Q,H}=N_{P,H}
. As subgroups of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
, this implies that
P_{H}\subset Q_{n}
,
M_{P,H}\subset M_{Q,n}
and
N_{P,H}\subset N_{Q,n}
, so that
P_{H}=Q_{n}
. By playing the same game in the
\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}
-coordinate, we get
P_{H}=Q_{n+1}\cap\mathrm{GL}_{n}
and therefore
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\RS}(P_{H})
. Conversely, we have described
\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{+}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{P_{H}}^{+}
in (
4.11
) in the case
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\RS}(P_{H})
and it is non-empty.
∎

Let
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. We have a basis
(e_{n+1,i}^{*})_{i}
of
\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{*}
(see §
3.1.1
). If
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}
, denote by
\lambda_{H}
its restriction to
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
. Set
e_{i}^{*}=(e_{n+1,i}^{*})_{H}
. This is zero if
i=i_{P}
. Then the family
(e_{i}^{*})_{i\neq i_{P}}
is a basis of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*}
the dual space of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
. Let
(e_{i})_{i}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
dual to
(e_{i}^{*})_{i\neq i_{P}}
(with the convention
e_{i_{P}}=0
).

Denote by
\Delta_{P_{n+1}}
the set
w_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}\Delta_{P^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{*}
, and by
\Delta_{P,H}
its restriction to
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
. Note that
\Delta_{P,H}
is a basis of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*}
. Let
\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{\vee}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
dual to
\Delta_{P,H}
. In coordinates, we have

\Delta_{P,H}=\left\{\frac{e_{i}^{*}}{n_{i}}-\frac{e_{i+1}^{*}}{n_{i+1}}\;\middle|\;1\leq i\leq m-1\right\},

(4.13)

where we recall that
e_{i_{P}}^{*}=0
, and

\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{\vee}=\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{i}n_{j}e_{j}\;\middle|\;i<i_{P}\right\}\bigcup\left\{-\sum_{j=i}^{m}n_{j}e_{j}\;\middle|\;i>i_{P}\right\}.

(4.14)

Then

\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}=\bigoplus_{\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{\vee}}\mathbb{R}_{>0}\varpi^{\vee}.

Recall that in §
3.1.1
we have given
\mathfrak{a}_{P}
an inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)
. By restricting, we obtain an inner product on
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
. Let
\Delta_{P,H}^{\vee}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
dual to
\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{\vee}
. Then we have

\Delta_{P,H}^{\vee}=\left\{e_{i}-e_{i+1}\;\middle|\;1\leq i\leq m-1\right\},

(4.15)

where again
e_{i_{P}}=0
. The inconsistency in the normalizations between (
4.13
) and (
4.15
) is due to the fact that
(e_{i},e_{i})=n_{i}^{-1}
. Set

\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*,+}=\left\{\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}\;\middle|\;\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}_{H}\rangle>0,\;\forall\alpha^{\vee}_{H}\in\Delta_{P,H}^{\vee}\right\}.

Note that
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}\subset\bigoplus_{\alpha^{\vee}_{H}\in\Delta_{P,H}^{\vee}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\alpha^{\vee}_{H}
, so that if
\lambda\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*,+}
we have
\langle\lambda,H\rangle>0
for every
H\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}
.

4.1.5
Relative constructions

Let
P\subset Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. Let
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}
be the orthogonal of
\mathfrak{z}_{Q}
in
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
. Note that this is consistent with our previous notation as
\mathfrak{z}_{G}=\{0\}
. If
T\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}
, let
T_{Q}
be its projection on
\mathfrak{z}_{Q}
and
T_{P}^{Q}
be its projection on
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}
, both with respect to
\mathfrak{z}_{P}=\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}\oplus\mathfrak{z}_{Q}
. This applies in particular to
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}=\mathfrak{z}_{P_{0}}
and we simply write
T^{Q}
for
T_{P_{0}}^{Q}
.

Let
\Delta_{P_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1}}
be the set
w_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}\Delta_{P^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{*}
and let
\Delta_{P,H}^{Q}
be its restriction to
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}
. Then
\Delta_{P,H}^{Q}
is a basis
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q,*}
. Let
\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}
dual to
\Delta_{P,H}^{Q}
, and let
\Delta_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}
dual to
\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}
under the restriction of the inner product. These sets are easy to describe. Indeed, using Lemma
4.3
we may write

\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}\oplus^{\perp}\mathfrak{z}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q}},

where we set
\mathbf{P}=P\cap\mathbf{M}_{Q}
(seen as a subgroup of
H)
and
\mathcal{P}=P\cap\mathcal{M}_{Q}
. It follows that we have decompositions
\Delta_{P,H}^{Q}=\Delta_{\mathbf{P},H}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}\sqcup\Delta_{\mathcal{P},H}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q}}
,
\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}=\hat{\Delta}_{\mathbf{P},H}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q},\vee}\sqcup\hat{\Delta}_{\mathcal{P},H}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q},\vee}
and
\Delta_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}=\Delta_{\mathbf{P},H}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q},\vee}\sqcup\Delta_{\mathcal{P},H}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q},\vee}
where
\Delta_{\mathbf{P},H}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}
,
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathbf{P},H}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q},\vee}
and
\Delta_{\mathbf{P},H}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q},\vee}
can be respectively identified with the usual sets of roots, coweights and coroots of the standard parabolic subgroup
\mathbf{P}
of
\mathbf{M}_{Q}
, and
\Delta_{\mathcal{P},H}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q}}
,
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathcal{P},H}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q},\vee}
and
\Delta_{\mathcal{P},H}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q},\vee}
are described by (
4.13
), (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) respectively.

4.1.6
The element
\underline{\rho}_{P}

Let
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. Set

\underline{\rho}_{P}=(\rho_{P}-2\rho_{P_{H}})_{|\mathfrak{z}_{P}}\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*}.

In coordinates, we have
(\underline{\rho}_{P})_{i}=\frac{1}{2}
if
i<i_{P}
, and
(\underline{\rho}_{P})_{i}=-\frac{1}{2}
if
i>i_{P}
.

Note that we have a surjection
\mathfrak{a}_{P^{\mathrm{std}}}^{*}\to\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*}
by composing with
w_{P}^{\mathrm{std},-1}
and then restricting. We now choose a lift
\underline{\rho}_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}
of
\underline{\rho}_{P}
. Under the decomposition of
M_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}
given in (
4.6
) and with the coordinates of §
3.1.1
, we set

\underline{\rho}_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}=\left((1/4,\ldots,1/4,0,-1/4,\ldots,-1/4),(1/4,\ldots,1/4,0,-1/4,\ldots,-1/4)\right),

(4.16)

where the zeros correspond to the blocks
\mathcal{M}_{P,n}
and
\mathcal{M}_{P,n+1}^{\mathrm{std}}
. Note that
\underline{\rho}_{P}^{\mathrm{std}}\in\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{P^{\mathrm{std}}}^{*,+}}
.

4.1.7
Volumes of fundamental domains

Let
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. Thanks to the isomorphism
\mathfrak{z}_{P}\simeq\oplus_{i\neq i_{P}}\mathfrak{a}_{\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}}
from (
4.10
), we give
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
the product of the Haar measures described in §
2.2.2
. If
P\subset Q
, we give
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}=\mathfrak{z}_{P}/\mathfrak{z}_{Q}
the quotient measure. If
B
is a basis of a lattice
\mathbb{Z}(B)
in
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}
, let
\mathrm{vol}(\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(B))
be its covolume. If
Q=G
, we have

\mathrm{vol}(\mathfrak{z}_{P}/\mathbb{Z}((e_{i})))=1,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{vol}(\mathfrak{z}_{P}/\mathbb{Z}(\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{\vee}))=\prod_{i\neq i_{P}}n_{i}.

(4.17)

Remark 4.6
.

In
[
58
, Section 4.3]
,
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
is given the measure associated to the Euclidean structure induced by
(\cdot,\cdot)
. One can check that it differs from our measure by a factor of
\prod_{i\neq i_{P}}n_{i}
. This normalization cancels out with the choice of measure we make in §
4.3.1
.

4.2
Exponential polynomials and Fourier transforms on cones

Let
V
be a finite dimensional vector space, let
\Omega\subset V
be an open subset. By an exponential polynomial on
\Omega
, we mean a function
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
which belongs to the vector space generated by the maps
v\mapsto P(v)\exp(\langle\lambda,v\rangle)
where
P\in\mathbb{C}[V]
and
\lambda\in V^{*}
. Any exponential polynomial can be uniquely written as
f(v)=\sum_{\lambda\in V^{*}}P_{\lambda}(v)\exp(\langle\lambda,v\rangle)
for a unique map
\lambda\mapsto P_{\lambda}
with finite support. The polynomial
P_{0}
is called the polynomial part of
f
.

Let
P\subset Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. Set

\varepsilon_{P}^{Q}=(-1)^{\dim\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}}=(-1)^{\dim\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1}}}.

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{*}
, set

\hat{\theta}_{P}^{Q}(\lambda)=\mathrm{vol}(\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}))^{-1}\prod_{\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}}\langle\lambda,\varpi^{\vee}\rangle,

and

\theta_{P}^{Q}(\lambda)=\mathrm{vol}(\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(\Delta_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}))^{-1}\prod_{\alpha^{\vee}_{H}\in\Delta_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}}\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}_{H}\rangle.

These polynomials are not identically zero on
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. In the special case where
P=P_{0}
, we omit the subscript
P
. We set

\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathbb{C}}^{*,Q-\mathrm{reg}}=\bigcap_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\left\{\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathbb{C}}^{*}\;\middle|\;\hat{\theta}_{P}^{Q}(\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{P})\neq 0\right\}.

(4.18)

If
R
is a semi-standard parabolic subgroup of
G
, we set
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\mathbb{C}}^{*,Q-\mathrm{reg}}=\mathfrak{a}_{R,\mathbb{C}}^{*}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathbb{C}}^{*,Q-\mathrm{reg}}
.

Remark 4.7
.

As noted in
[
58
, Section 4.5]
, it is enough to take the intersection in (
4.18
) along maximal proper Rankin–Selberg parabolic subgroups
P
of
Q
. In that case, the condition amounts to asking that
\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{P}
is non-zero when restricted to
\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}
.

We define as in
[
58
, 7]

\displaystyle A(\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}},\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}})=\left\{r(H-Z)\;|\;H\in\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}},\;r>0\right\},

\displaystyle A(\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}},\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}})^{\vee}=\{H\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}\;|\;(H,H^{\prime})\geq 0,\;H^{\prime}\in A(\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}},\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}})\},

where
Z
is any
Z\in\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}
. We have

\displaystyle A(\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}},\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}})=\left\{H\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}\;|\;\forall\alpha\in\Delta_{P,H}^{Q},\;\langle\alpha,H\rangle\geq 0\right\}=\bigoplus_{\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\varpi^{\vee}\bigoplus\mathfrak{z}_{Q},

(4.19)

\displaystyle A(\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}},\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}})^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{\alpha_{H}^{\vee}\in\Delta_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\alpha^{\vee}_{H}.

(4.20)

Let
\tau_{P}^{Q}
be the characteristic function of
A(\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}},\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}})
, and let
\hat{\tau}_{P}^{Q}
be the characteristic function of
A(\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{Q}^{+}},\overline{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{+}})^{\vee}
. These two functions are defined on
\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
. Set

\Gamma_{P}^{Q}(H,T)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset R\subset Q\end{subarray}}\varepsilon_{R}^{Q}\tau_{P}^{R}(H)\hat{\tau}_{R}^{Q}(H-T),\quad H,T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}.

We now compute the Fourier transforms of these functions.

Lemma 4.8
.

1.

For every polynomial
q\in\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{z}_{P}]
, the Fourier transform

\mathrm{FT}_{P}^{Q}(q,\lambda):=\int_{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}}\tau_{P}^{Q}(H)q(H)\exp(\langle\lambda,H\rangle)dh,

(4.21)

is absolutely convergent in the open subset
\cap_{\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{Q,\vee}}\{\langle\Re(\lambda),\varpi^{\vee}\rangle<0\}
and extends to a meromorphic function on
\{\lambda\;|\;\hat{\theta}_{P}^{Q}(\lambda)\neq 0\}
.

2.

For every
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
the map
H\mapsto\Gamma_{P}^{Q}(H,T)
is compactly supported with support contained in the open ball
B(T/2,\left\lVert T\right\rVert/2)\subset\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
. Let
q\in\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{z}_{P}]
. For fixed
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
the Fourier transform

\mathrm{FT}_{P}^{Q}(\Gamma,T,q,\lambda):=\int_{\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{Q}}\Gamma_{P}^{Q}(H,T)q(H)\exp(\langle\lambda,H\rangle)dH

is an exponential polynomial in
T
, and for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{Q,*}
such that
\hat{\theta}_{P}^{Q}(\lambda)\neq 0
its purely polynomial term is constant equal to
\mathrm{FT}_{P}^{Q}(q,\lambda)
.

3.

If
q=1
, we have

\mathrm{FT}_{P}^{Q}(1,\lambda)=\varepsilon_{P}^{Q}\hat{\theta}_{P}^{Q}(\lambda)^{-1}.

(4.22)

and for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{Q,*}
in general position

\mathrm{FT}_{P}^{Q}(\Gamma,T,1,\lambda)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset R\subset Q\end{subarray}}\varepsilon_{P}^{R}\hat{\theta}_{P}^{R}(\lambda)^{-1}\theta_{R}^{Q}(\lambda)^{-1}\exp(\langle\lambda,T_{R}^{Q}\rangle).

(4.23)

Proof.

The first two points are contained in
[
58
, Section 1.4]
and
[
57
, Corollary 2.6]
. For the last, note that the set of
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
such that, for all
P\subset R\subset Q
, all
\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{P,H}^{R,\vee}
,
\langle\Re(\lambda),\varpi^{\vee}\rangle<0
and all
\alpha^{\vee}_{H}\in\Delta_{R,H}^{Q,\vee}
,
\langle\Re(\lambda),\alpha^{\vee}_{H}\rangle<0
, is non-empty. Indeed, it contains the
\lambda
’s such that the restriction of
\Re(\lambda)
to
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
belongs to
-\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*,+}
. For such
\lambda
, we see using (
4.19
) and (
4.20
) that (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) hold. We conclude that they hold for
\lambda
in general position by analytic continuation.
∎

4.3
Regularized Rankin–Selberg periods à la Zydor

In this section, we fix
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. We write
M^{\mathrm{std}}_{Q,n+1}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}
. Let
T_{H,\mathrm{reg}}\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
be the element defined in
[
58
, Lemma 2.7]
. We say that
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
is sufficiently positive if
T\in T_{H,\mathrm{reg}}+\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}^{+}
. We now define the truncated period
\mathcal{P}^{T}
and its regularization
\mathcal{P}
.

4.3.1
Iwasawa decomposition and measures

Set

M_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})^{Q,1}=\left\{m\in M_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})\;\middle|\;\left(H_{Q_{H}}(m)\right)_{Q}=0\right\},\quad Z_{Q}^{\infty}=A_{Q}^{\infty}\cap A_{Q_{H}}^{\infty}.

The restriction of
H_{Q_{H}}
to
Z_{Q}^{\infty}
is an isomorphism with image
\mathfrak{z}_{Q}
. This gives
Z_{Q}^{\infty}
a Haar measure. We have a direct product decomposition of commuting groups
M_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})=Z_{Q}^{\infty}M_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})^{Q,1}
. By the Iwasawa decomposition, there is a unique Haar measure on
M_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})^{Q,1}
such that for all
f\in C_{c}(H(\mathbb{A}))
we have

\int_{H(\mathbb{A})}f(h)dh=\int_{K_{H}}\int_{N_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})}\int_{M_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})^{Q,1}}\int_{Z_{Q}^{\infty}}\exp(\langle-2\rho_{Q_{H}},H_{Q_{H}}(am)\rangle)f(nmak)dadmdndk.

(4.24)

We set

[M_{Q_{H}}]^{Q,1}=M_{Q_{H}}(F)\backslash M_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})^{Q,1}

which is given the quotient by the counting measure. Note that we have

[M_{Q,H}]^{Q,1}\simeq\left(\prod_{i\neq i_{Q}}[\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}]^{1}\right)\times[\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{Q}}-1}]=[\mathbf{M}_{Q}]^{1}\times[\mathcal{M}_{Q,H}].

Then the measure
dh
coincides the product of the ones on each
[\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}]^{1}
and on
[\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i_{Q}}-1}]
described in §
2.2.2
. Moreover, if
Q=G
we have
M_{G,H}(\mathbb{A})^{G,1}=H(\mathbb{A})
and
Z_{G}^{\infty}=\{1\}
.

4.3.2
Truncation operators

Let
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
. For
\phi
a locally integrable function on
Q(F)\backslash G(\mathbb{A})
, we define a relative truncation operator as

\Lambda^{T,Q}\phi(h)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\varepsilon_{P}^{Q}\sum_{\delta\in P_{H}(F)\backslash Q_{H}(F)}\hat{\tau}_{P}^{Q}(H_{0,H}(\delta h)^{Q}_{P}-T^{Q}_{P})\phi_{P}(\delta h),\quad h\in Q_{H}(F)\backslash H(\mathbb{A}),

(4.25)

where the sum over
\delta
is actually over a finite set which only depends on
h
and
T
by
[
58
, Lemma 2.8]
. If
Q=G
we simply write
\Lambda^{T}
. The main theorem on this operator is the following.

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
be sufficiently positive. Let
J
be a level of
G
. For any
N,N^{\prime}>0
there exists a finite family
(X_{i})_{i\in I}
of elements in
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\infty})
such that for any smooth and right
J
-invariant function
\phi
on
[G]_{Q}
, the function
\Lambda^{T,Q}\phi
is a function on
[H]_{Q_{H}}
and we have

\sup_{m\in M_{Q,H}(\mathbb{A})^{Q,1}}\left\lVert m\right\rVert^{N}_{M_{Q,H}}\left|\Lambda^{T,Q}\phi(mk)\right|\leq\sum_{i\in I}\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{-N^{\prime},X_{i}}.

Proof.

This is
[
58
, Theorem 3.9]
. Note that the statement in ibid. is weaker, but our version can easily be extracted from the proof.
∎

The operator
\Lambda^{T,Q}
also satisfies two unfolding properties.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\phi
be a locally integrable function on
Q(F)\backslash G(\mathbb{A})
. For all
T,T^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
and all
h\in H(\mathbb{A})
we have

\phi(h)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\sum_{\delta\in P_{H}(F)\backslash Q_{H}(F)}\tau_{P}^{Q}(H_{0,H}(\delta h)_{P}^{Q}-T_{P}^{Q})\Lambda^{T,P}\phi(\delta h),

(4.26)

and

\Lambda^{T+T^{\prime},Q}\phi(h)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\sum_{\delta\in P_{H}(F)\backslash Q_{H}(F)}\Gamma_{P}^{Q}(H_{0,H}(\delta h)_{P}^{Q}-T_{P}^{Q},(T^{\prime})_{P}^{Q})\Lambda^{T,P}\phi(\delta h).

(4.27)

Proof.

These formulae are
[
58
, Lemma 3.7]
and
[
58
, Lemma 3.8]
. Note that they are only stated there for
Q=G
. However, the proof ultimately relies on combinatorics of cones and therefore goes through in the general case.
∎

4.3.3
Truncated periods

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{T}([G])
. We have
\phi_{Q}\in\mathcal{T}([G]_{Q})
. For
T
sufficiently positive, we define the truncated period of
\phi
relative to
Q
to be

\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\phi)=\int_{K_{H}}\int_{[M_{Q,H}]^{Q,1}}\exp(-\langle 2\rho_{P_{H}},H_{P_{H}}(m)\rangle)\Lambda^{T,Q}\phi(mk)dmdk.

This integral is absolutely convergent by Theorem
4.9
and
[
11
, Proposition A.1.1]
. Note that if
Q=G
it reduces to

\mathcal{P}^{T}(\phi)=\int_{[H]}\Lambda^{T}\phi(h)dh.

4.3.4
Regularized periods

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{Q}(G)
be an automorphic form and let
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
which is contained in
Q
. By §
2.4
, we have a decomposition of
\varphi_{P}
as sum of normalized automorphic forms in
\mathcal{A}_{P}^{0}(G)
multiplied by exponents. By reorganizing, we see that we also have a decomposition
(where all terms are non-zero) such that

\varphi_{P}(ah)=\sum_{i}q_{P,i}(H_{P_{H}}(a))\exp(\langle\lambda_{P,i},H_{P_{H}}(a)\rangle)\varphi_{P,i}(h),\quad a\in Z_{P}^{\infty},\quad h\in N_{P_{H}}(\mathbb{A})M_{P,H}(\mathbb{A})^{P,1}K_{H},

(4.28)

with
q_{P,i}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{z}_{P}]
,
\lambda_{P,i}\in\mathfrak{z}_{P}^{*}
, and
\varphi_{P,i}\in\mathcal{A}_{P}(G)
that are left
Z_{P}^{\infty}
-invariant. Let
\mathcal{E}_{P,H}(\varphi)=\{\lambda_{P,i}\}
. Note that this is the restriction to
\mathfrak{z}_{P}
of the elements in the set of exponents
\mathcal{E}_{P}(\varphi)
from §
2.4
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{Q}(G)^{\mathrm{reg}}
be the subspace of
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{Q}(G)
such that for all
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
with
P\subset Q
we have

\mathcal{E}_{P}(\varphi)\subset\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*,Q-\mathrm{reg}}.

(4.29)

The subspace
\mathcal{A}_{Q}(G)^{\mathrm{reg}}
is stable under right-translations by
G(\mathbb{A})
.

Theorem 4.11
.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{Q}(G)^{\mathrm{reg}}
there exists a unique exponential polynomial on
\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
that coincides with
T\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\varphi)
for
T
sufficiently positive. Its purely polynomial part is constant and equal to

\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\sum_{i}\exp(\langle\lambda_{P,i}+\underline{\rho}_{P},T_{P}^{Q}\rangle)\mathrm{FT}_{P}^{Q}((q_{P,i})_{T},\lambda_{P,i}+\underline{\rho}_{P})\mathcal{P}^{T,P}(\varphi_{P,i}),

(4.30)

where
(q_{P,i})_{T}(H)=q_{P,i}(H+T_{P}^{Q})
. Moreover,
\mathcal{P}^{G}
is
H(\mathbb{A})
-invariant.

Proof.

The
Q=G
case is
[
58
, Theorem 4.1]
. For the general case, using Lemma
4.8
, the Iwasawa decomposition (
4.24
) and Lemma
4.10
we have for any for
T
sufficiently positive and
T^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}^{+}}
the expression

\mathcal{P}^{T+T^{\prime},Q}(\varphi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\sum_{i}\exp(\langle\lambda_{P,i}+\underline{\rho}_{P},T_{P}^{Q}\rangle)\mathrm{FT}_{P}^{Q}(\Gamma,T^{\prime},(q_{P,i})_{T},\lambda_{P,i}+\underline{\rho}_{P})\mathcal{P}^{T,P}(\varphi_{P,i}).

(4.31)

We now conclude by Lemma
4.8
.
∎

The number
\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi)
is the
regularized period à la Zydor
along
Z_{Q}^{\infty}M_{Q_{H}}(F)N_{Q_{H}}(\mathbb{A})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})
.

4.3.5
Parabolic descent

We describe
\Lambda^{T,Q}
by parabolic descent. Set
P_{0,\mathbf{Q}}:=P_{0}\cap\mathbf{M}_{Q}
, and
\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathbf{Q}}:=\mathfrak{a}_{P_{0,\mathbf{Q}}}
. For every standard parabolic subgroup
\mathbf{P}
of
\mathbf{M}_{Q}
(with respect to
P_{0,\mathbf{Q}}
), let
\hat{\tau}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}_{\mathbf{P}}
be the characteristic function of

\left\{H\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathbf{Q}}\;\middle|\;\langle\varpi,H\rangle\geq 0,\;\forall\varpi\in\hat{\Delta}_{\mathbf{P}}\right\}.

Let
\mathbf{T}\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}
. For
\phi
a locally integrable function on
[\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}]
, define the diagonal Arthur truncation operator to be

\Lambda^{T,\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}\phi(m)=\sum_{\mathbf{P}\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}(P_{0})}(-1)^{\dim\mathfrak{a}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}}\sum_{\delta\in\mathbf{P}(F)\backslash\mathbf{M}_{Q}(F)}\hat{\tau}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}_{\mathbf{P}}(H_{0}(\delta m)-\mathbf{T})\phi_{\mathbf{P}^{2}}(\delta m,\delta m),\;m\in[\mathbf{M}_{Q}],

(4.32)

where
\mathbf{P}^{2}
is the standard parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}
equal to
\mathbf{P}\times\mathbf{P}
. Note that
\Lambda^{T,\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}
is the truncation operator built in
[
58
, Section 3.7]
relatively to the diagonal embedding
\mathbf{M}_{Q}\subset\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}
. In particular it takes smooth functions of uniform moderate growth on
[\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}]
to functions of rapid decay on
[\mathbf{M}_{Q}]^{1}
as in Theorem
4.9
. We set

\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}(\phi)=\int_{[\mathbf{M}_{Q}]^{1}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}\phi(m)dm,

The operator
\Lambda^{T,\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}
differs from the usual Arthur truncation operator of
[
3
]
. Indeed, in the formalism of
[
58
]
, the latter (say for some
\mathrm{GL}_{k}
) is associated to
\mathrm{GL}_{k}\subset\mathrm{GL}_{k}
and not
\mathrm{GL}_{k}\subset\mathrm{GL}_{k}^{2}
.

By
[
58
, Theorem 4.1]
,
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}
gives rise to a regularized period
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}
when restricted to
\mathcal{A}(\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2})^{\mathrm{reg}}
the subset of
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}(\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2})
such that for all standard parabolic subgroup
\mathbf{P}
of
\mathbf{M}_{Q}

\forall\lambda\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{P}^{2}}(\varphi),\;\forall\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\vee},\;\langle\lambda,\varpi^{\vee}\rangle\neq 0.

(4.33)

If
\phi\in\mathcal{A}(\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2})^{\mathrm{reg}}
, then the regularized period
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}(\phi)
satisfies a relation of the form

\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}(\phi)=\sum_{\mathbf{P}\subset\mathbf{M}_{Q}\;\mathrm{standard}}\sum_{i}\exp(\langle\lambda_{\mathbf{P},i},\mathbf{T}_{\mathbf{P}}\rangle)\mathrm{FT}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}((q_{\mathbf{P},i})_{\mathbf{T}},\lambda_{\mathbf{P},i})\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{P}}(\phi_{\mathbf{P},i}),

(4.34)

where the
\lambda_{\mathbf{P},i}
,
q_{\mathbf{P},i}
and
\phi_{\mathbf{P},i}
are respectively the exponents, polynomials and automorphic forms appearing the normalized decomposition of
\phi_{\mathbf{P}^{2}}
, and
\mathrm{FT}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}
is a Fourier transform defined as in Lemma
4.8
(see
[
58
, Section 4.4]
).

Set
P_{0,\mathcal{Q}}:=P_{0}\cap\mathcal{M}_{Q,H}
, and
\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}:=\mathfrak{a}_{P_{0,\mathcal{Q}}}
. Let
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}
. For
\phi
a locally integrable function on
[\mathcal{M}_{Q}]
, we define
\Lambda^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}\phi
to be the operator
\Lambda^{\mathcal{T}}
of (
4.25
) built with respect to
\mathcal{M}_{Q,H}\subset\mathcal{M}_{Q}
. We then have a truncated period
\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}(\phi)
and a regularized period
\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q}}(\phi)
, the latter being well defined if
\phi\in\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{M}_{Q})^{\mathrm{reg}}
where this set is described as in (
4.29
).

The behavior of the regularized period
\mathcal{P}^{Q}
is summarized in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{Q}(G)^{\mathrm{reg}}
. Set

\varphi_{M_{Q}}=\left(R(e_{K_{H}})\varphi\right)_{|M_{Q}(\mathbb{A}),-\rho_{Q}}.

Then we have

\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}\left(m\in[\mathbf{M}^{2}_{Q}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q}}(R(m)\varphi_{M_{Q}})\right)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q}}\left(m\in[\mathcal{M}_{Q}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}(R(m)\varphi_{M_{Q}})\right).

(4.35)

Proof.

For any
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}
we have a unique decomposition
T^{Q}=\mathbf{T}+\mathcal{T}
where
\mathbf{T}\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}}
and
\mathcal{T}\in\mathfrak{a}_{0,\mathcal{Q}}
. By Lemma
4.3
, it is easily checked that we have for every
\mathbf{m}\in[\mathbf{M}_{Q}]
and
m\in[\mathcal{M}_{Q,H}]

\displaystyle\Lambda^{T,Q}\varphi(\mathbf{m}m)

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}\left(m^{\prime}\in[\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}]\mapsto\Lambda^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}(R(m^{\prime})\varphi)(m)\right)(\mathbf{m})

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}\left(m^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{M}_{Q}]\mapsto\Lambda^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}}(R(m^{\prime})\varphi)(\mathbf{m})\right)(m).

If
\phi
is a locally integrable function on
[\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}]
, we know that the sum defining
\Lambda^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}\phi(m)
is over a finite set depending only on
\mathbf{T}
and
m
and not on
\phi
(
[
58
, Lemma 2.8]
). The same is true with the other truncation operator
\Lambda^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}
. We may therefore switch
\Lambda^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}
with the integrals over
[\mathcal{M}_{Q,H}]
and
K_{H}
(or
\Lambda^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}
with those on
[\mathbf{M}_{Q}]^{1}
and
K_{H}
) and see that
\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\varphi)
is

\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}\left(m\in[\mathbf{M}^{2}_{Q}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}(R(m)\varphi_{M_{Q}})\right)=\mathcal{P}^{\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{Q}}\left(m\in[\mathcal{M}_{Q}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{T},\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}(R(m)\varphi_{M_{Q}})\right)

It remains to say that the regularized periods can be expressed in terms of the truncated ones (Theorem
4.11
and (
4.34
)) to conclude.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

Note that if
\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}^{2}
and if
\phi\in\mathcal{A}(\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2})^{\mathrm{reg}}
, we may further decompose

\mathcal{P}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q}^{2}}(\phi)=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}^{2}}(\phi),

(4.36)

where
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}^{2}}
is the regularized diagonal Arthur period along
\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}
. Moreover, by
[
58
, Theorem 4.6]
we know that, when applied to a discrete automorphic form of some
\mathrm{GL}_{k}^{2}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{GL}_{k}^{2}}
computes the Petersson (bilinear) inner-product
\langle\cdot,\bar{\cdot}\rangle_{\mathrm{GL}_{k},\mathrm{Pet}}
.

4.3.6
Regularized periods of Eisenstein series

Let
P
be a standard parabolic subgroup of
G
, let
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathrm{disc}}(M_{P})
. Recall that such
\pi
has trivial central character on
A_{P}^{\infty}
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{P,\pi}(G)
. Let
w\in{}_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{P}
. For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{w},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position, set

\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w)=\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(E^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}(w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\cdot,M(w,\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w}},w\lambda)),

(4.37)

Remark 4.14
.

The element
w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}
was defined in (
4.5
). We prefer to make it appear in (
4.37
) to deal with the standard parabolic subgroup
Q^{\mathrm{std}}
rather than
Q
.

The truncated period in (
4.37
) is well defined for
\lambda
in general position by
[
46
, Theorem 2.2]
(which ensures that generalized Eisenstein series are of uniform moderate growth), and if
P_{\pi}\not\subset P_{w}
it is zero. Let us compute the exponents of the generalized Eisenstein series. Let
R\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
. By (
2.5
) and Lemma
4.3
we have

\left(E^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}(w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\cdot,M(w,\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w}},w\lambda)\right)_{R}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}w\\
P_{\pi}\subset P_{w^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}E^{R^{\mathrm{std}}}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\cdot,M(w^{\prime},\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w^{\prime}}},w^{\prime}\lambda).

(4.38)

Let
w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}w
such that
P_{\pi}\subset P_{w^{\prime}}
. Denote by
(w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}}))_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}
the projection of
w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})
on
\mathfrak{a}_{R^{\mathrm{std}},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. Then by Lemma
3.2
we know that

\left(E^{R^{\mathrm{std}}}(M(w^{\prime},\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w^{\prime}}},w^{\prime}\lambda)_{-(w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}}))_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}}\right)(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\cdot)\in\mathcal{A}_{R}^{0}(G).

(4.39)

Because
\mathfrak{z}_{R}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{R}
, it follows that the exponents of (
4.38
) are the

\left(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}\left(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}}\right)\right)_{|\mathfrak{z}_{R}}.

(4.40)

We will see in Lemma
4.15
that they are all contained in
\mathfrak{a}_{R,\mathbb{C}}^{*,Q-\mathrm{reg}}
for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position. Therefore, for such
\lambda
we set

\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w)=\mathcal{P}^{Q}(E^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}(w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\cdot,M(w,\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w}},w\lambda)).

(4.41)

Lemma 4.15
.

For every
R\subset Q
and every
w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}w
such that
P_{\pi}\subset P_{w^{\prime}}
we have for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position

\hat{\theta}_{R}^{Q}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})\theta_{R}^{Q}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})\neq 0

Proof.

By Lemma
2.1
we have
w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{P}
. Set
\mathbf{R}=R\cap\mathbf{M}_{Q}
and
\mathcal{R}=R\cap\mathcal{M}_{Q}
. Write
P=P_{n}\times P_{n+1}
and
\lambda=\lambda_{n}+\lambda_{n+1}
,
w=(w_{n},w_{n+1})
accordingly. Let
\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{R,H}^{Q,\vee}
. By §
4.1.5
we have two cases.

1.

\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{\mathbf{R},H}^{\mathbf{M}_{Q},\vee}
: in that case we have
\langle\underline{\rho}_{R},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle=0
and there exist
\varpi_{n}^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{R_{n}}^{Q_{n},\vee}
and
\varpi_{n+1}^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1},\vee}
such that

\langle w^{\prime}\lambda+w^{\prime}\nu_{w^{\prime}},w_{R}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\varpi^{\vee}\rangle=\langle w^{\prime}_{n}\lambda_{n}+w^{\prime}_{n}\nu_{w^{\prime}_{n}},\varpi_{n}^{\vee}\rangle+\langle w^{\prime}_{n+1}\lambda_{n+1}+w^{\prime}_{n+1}\nu_{w^{\prime}_{n+1}},\varpi^{\vee}_{n+1}\rangle.

By
[
20
, Lemma 3.1.5.1]
and
[
20
, Lemma 3.1.5.2]
, because
P_{\pi}\subset P_{w^{\prime}}
if this expression is constant then it is strictly negative.

2.

\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{\mathcal{R},H}^{\mathcal{M}_{Q},\vee}
: in that case by (
4.14
)
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}\mapsto\langle w^{\prime}\lambda,w_{R}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\varpi^{\vee}\rangle
is clearly non-zero.

This shows that
\hat{\theta}_{R}^{Q}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})\neq 0
for
\lambda
in general position. The argument for
\theta_{R}^{Q}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})
is the same.
∎

Proposition 4.16
.

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position and
T
sufficiently positive we have

\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
R\subset Q\end{subarray}}\varepsilon_{R}^{Q}\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}w}\mathcal{P}^{T,R}(\varphi,\lambda,w^{\prime})\cdot\frac{\exp(\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R},T_{R}^{Q}\rangle)}{\hat{\theta}_{R}^{Q}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})},

(4.42)

and

\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}\\
R\subset Q\end{subarray}}\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}w}\mathcal{P}^{R}(\varphi,\lambda,w^{\prime})\cdot\frac{\exp(\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R},T_{R}^{Q}\rangle)}{\theta_{R}^{Q}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})},

(4.43)

where in both cases we understand that the summands are zero unless
P_{\pi}\subset P_{w^{\prime}}
(whether or not the denominator is identically zero).

Proof.

The first point follows from (
4.30
) by the computation of the constant term in (
4.38
) and of the exponents in (
4.39
). Indeed, we know by Lemma
4.15
that for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position

E^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}(w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\cdot,M(w,\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w}},w\lambda)\in\mathcal{A}_{Q}(G)^{\mathrm{reg}},

and by Lemma
4.8
we have
\mathrm{FT}_{R}^{Q}=\varepsilon_{R}^{Q}(\hat{\theta}_{R}^{Q})^{-1}
.

We prove (
4.43
). To ease notation, we assume that
Q=G
and
w=1
, the general case being similar. By the computation of the constant term in (
4.38
), we see by (
4.31
) that for
T
sufficiently positive,
T^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{0,H}^{+}}
and
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position,
\mathcal{P}^{T+T^{\prime}}(\varphi,\lambda)
is

\sum_{R\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}}\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{P}}\exp(\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R},T_{R}\rangle)\mathrm{FT}_{R}^{G}(\Gamma,T^{\prime},1,w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})\mathcal{P}^{T,R}(\varphi,\lambda,w)

By Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.15
, for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position we have

\mathrm{FT}_{R}^{G}(\Gamma,T^{\prime},1,w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})=\sum_{Q\supset R}\varepsilon_{R}^{Q}\frac{\exp(\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R},T^{\prime}_{Q}\rangle)}{(\hat{\theta}_{R}^{Q}\theta_{Q})(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})}.

Note that if
w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}w
for some
w\in{}_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{P}
, for any
H\in\mathfrak{z}_{Q}
we have by Lemma
4.3

\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R},H\rangle=\langle w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}w(\lambda+\nu_{w})+\underline{\rho}_{Q},H\rangle.

By switching the sums over
Q
and
R
and using Lemma
2.1
we now see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{T+T^{\prime}}(\varphi,\lambda)=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}}\sum_{w\in{}_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{P}}\frac{\exp(\langle w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}(\lambda+\nu_{w})+\underline{\rho}_{R},T_{Q}+T^{\prime}_{Q}\rangle)}{\theta_{Q}(w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}w(\lambda+\nu_{w})+\underline{\rho}_{Q})}\times

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{R\subset Q}\varepsilon_{R}^{Q}\sum_{w^{\prime}\in{}_{R^{\mathrm{std}}}W_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}w}\mathcal{P}^{T,R}(\varphi,\lambda,w^{\prime})\cdot\frac{\exp(\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R},T_{R}^{Q}\rangle)}{\hat{\theta}_{R}^{Q}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w^{\prime}(\lambda+\nu_{w^{\prime}})+\underline{\rho}_{R})}\right).

By the first part of the theorem, the last line is
\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w^{\prime})
. We conclude by taking
T^{\prime}=0
.
∎

Corollary 4.17
.

The map
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w)
is meromorphic. Moreover, let
f
be a holomorphic function such that

\lambda\mapsto M^{*}(w,\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w}}:=f(\lambda)M(w,\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w}}

is holomorphic in a neighborhood of
\lambda_{0}
. Then the map

\lambda\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{Q,*}(\varphi,\lambda,w):=f(\lambda)\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w)

is meromorphic and we have in a neighborhood of
\lambda_{0}

\mathcal{P}^{Q,*}(\varphi,\lambda,w)=\mathcal{P}^{Q}(M^{*}(w,\lambda)\varphi_{P_{w}},\lambda,w).

(4.44)

Proof.

By Theorem
4.9
and the continuity of Eisenstein series from
[
46
, Theorem 2.2]
, for every
T
sufficiently positive the map
\lambda\mapsto\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w)
is meromorphic. The first statement therefore follows from Lemma
4.15
and Proposition
4.16
. The other assertions are proved by the same argument.
∎

4.4
Regularized periods and Zeta functions

In this section, we give a representation-theoretic interpretation of the regularized period
\mathcal{P}^{Q}
. By Lemma
4.12
and Remark
4.13
, it is enough to understand Zydor’s regularized period
\mathcal{P}
. We show that
\mathcal{P}
it coincides with the Ichino–Yamana regularized period
\mathcal{P}^{IY}
built in
[
33
]
. It follows from
[
33
, Theorem 1.1]
that it computes the Zeta integral of automorphic forms.

4.4.1
Local Whittaker functionals

Let
\psi
be a non-trivial automorphic unitary character of
F\backslash\mathbb{A}
. We define
\psi_{0}
a generic automorphic character of
N_{0}(\mathbb{A})
by the formula

\psi_{0}(u)=\psi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}-(u_{n})_{i,i+1}\right)\psi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(u_{n+1})_{i,i+1}\right),\quad u\in N_{0}(\mathbb{A}).

This is a generic character of
N_{0}(\mathbb{A})
which is trivial on
N_{0,H}(\mathbb{A})
. It decomposes as
\psi_{0}=\prod_{v}\psi_{0,v}
.

We now fix
v
a place of
F
. Let
P
be a standard parabolic subgroup of
G
, and let
\pi_{v}
be a smooth irreducible representation of
M_{P}(F_{v})
. We say that
\pi_{v}
is generic if it admits a non-zero Whittaker model with respect to the restriction of
\psi_{0,v}
to
M_{P}(F_{v})\cap N_{0}(F_{v})
(in which case it is for every generic character). We denote by
\mathcal{W}(\pi_{v},\psi_{0,v})
the Whittaker model of
\pi_{v}
, and take an isomorphism
W_{\pi_{v}}^{\psi_{0,v}}:\pi_{v}\to\mathcal{W}(\pi,\psi_{0,v})
.

Let
w
be the element in the Weyl group of
G
such that
w(\Delta_{0}^{P})\subset\Delta_{0}
and such that for every root
\alpha
in
N_{P}
we have
w\alpha<0
. Let
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}N^{\prime}
be the standard parabolic subgroup of
G
with standard Levi factor
wM_{P}w^{-1}
. Let
w\psi_{0}
be the generic character of
(M_{P}\cap N_{0})(\mathbb{A})
such that for
n^{\prime}\in(M_{P^{\prime}}\cap N_{0})(\mathbb{A})
we have
w\psi_{0}(\dot{w}^{-1}n^{\prime}\dot{w})=\psi_{0}(n^{\prime})
. Let
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. For
\varphi_{v}\in I_{P}^{G}(\pi_{v,\lambda})
with support in
P(F_{v})\dot{w}P_{0}(F_{v})
, we define the
Jacquet functional
by

W^{\psi_{0,v}}_{P,\pi_{v}}(\varphi_{v},\lambda)=\int_{N^{\prime}(F_{v})}W^{w\psi_{0,v}}_{\pi_{v}}(\varphi_{\lambda,v}(\dot{w}^{-1}n^{\prime}_{v}))\overline{\psi_{0,v}}(n^{\prime}_{v})dn^{\prime}_{v}.

(4.45)

Then
W^{\psi_{0,v}}_{P,\pi_{v}}(\cdot,\lambda)
extends to a linear form on
I_{P}^{G}(\pi_{v,\lambda})
. We also write
W^{\psi_{0,v}}_{P,\pi_{v}}
for the associated Whittaker function, i.e.

W^{\psi_{0,v}}_{P,\pi_{v}}(g_{v},\varphi_{v},\lambda)=W^{\psi_{0,v}}_{P,\pi_{v}}(R(g_{v})\varphi_{v},\lambda).

Moreover, the map
\lambda\mapsto W^{\psi_{0,v}}_{P,\pi_{v}}(g_{v},\varphi_{v},\lambda)
is holomorphic (see
[
16
]
).

4.4.2
Local Zeta functions

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
with
\Re(\lambda)
in a positive cone, we define the local Zeta function by

Z_{\pi_{v}}(\varphi_{v},\lambda)=\int_{N_{0,H}(F_{v})\backslash H(F_{v})}W^{\psi_{0,v}}_{P,\pi_{v}}(h_{v},\varphi_{v},\lambda)dh_{v},\quad\varphi_{v}\in I_{P}^{G}\pi_{v}.

(4.46)

It admits a meromorphic continuation to
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
.

Write
M_{P}=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{n}}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{n,i}}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{n+1}}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{n+1,i}}
and
\pi_{v}=\boxtimes_{i=1}^{m_{n}}\pi_{v,n,i}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{m_{n+1}}\pi_{v,n+1,i}
accordingly. For every place
v
, set

b(\lambda,\pi_{v})=\prod_{i<j}L(1+\lambda_{n,i}-\lambda_{n,j},\pi_{v,n,i}\times\pi_{v,n,j}^{\vee})\prod_{i<j}L(1+\lambda_{n+1,i}-\lambda_{n+1,j},\pi_{v,n+1,i}\times\pi_{v,n+1,j}^{\vee}).

(4.47)

Moreover, consider the Rankin–Selberg
L
-function

L\left(\lambda+\frac{1}{2},\pi_{v,n}\times\pi_{v,n+1}\right):=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{m_{n+1}}L(1/2+\lambda_{n,i}+\lambda_{n+1,j},\pi_{v,n,i}\times\pi_{v,n+1,j}).

(4.48)

We now define the
normalized local Zeta function
by

Z_{\pi_{v}}^{\natural}(\varphi_{v},\lambda):=b(\lambda,\pi_{v})\frac{Z_{\pi_{v}}(\varphi_{v},\lambda)}{L\left(\lambda+1/2,\pi_{v,n}\times\pi_{v,n+1}\right)}.

Lemma 4.18
.

For any
\varphi_{v}\in I_{P}^{G}\pi_{v}
, the quotient

\lambda\mapsto\frac{Z_{\pi_{v}}(\varphi_{v},\lambda)}{L\left(\lambda+1/2,\pi_{v,n}\times\pi_{v,n+1}\right)}

(4.49)

is holomorphic on
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. If we assume moreover that
\pi_{v}
is unitary, the regularized Zeta function
\lambda\mapsto Z_{\pi_{v}}^{\natural}(\varphi_{v},\lambda)
is a meromorphic function on
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
which is regular in the region
\Re(\lambda)\in\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*,+}}
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that (
4.49
) is not regular on
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. Then there exists some
\mu
such that there is a single polar divisor passing through
\mu
and no zero divisor. Set
W_{v}=W_{P,\pi_{v}}^{\psi_{0,v}}(\cdot,\varphi_{v},\mu)
which belongs to the Whittaker model of
I_{P}^{G}\pi_{v,\mu}
. Assume first that
v
is non-Archimedean. By
[
34
, Theorem 2.7]
, there exists a meromorphic function
L(s+1/2,I_{P}^{G}\pi_{v,\mu})
such that the quotient

s\mapsto\frac{Z_{\pi_{v}}(W_{v},s)}{L(s+1/2,I_{P}^{G}\pi_{v,\mu})}

is entire, where
Z_{\pi_{v}}(W_{v},s)
is the Zeta function from (
4.46
) associated to
W_{v}
. By
[
34
, Theorem 3.1]
,
L(s+1/2,I_{P}^{G}\pi_{v,\mu})
is equal to the product of
L\left(1/2+s+\lambda,\pi_{v,n}\times\pi_{v,n+1}\right)
(where we identify
s
with an element in
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathrm{GL}_{n},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
) with a polynomial in
q_{v}^{s}
and
q_{v}^{-s}
. Therefore, (
4.49
) is also entire. This is the desired contradiction as the singularity at
\mu
cannot be compensated by a zero by assumption. In the Archimedean case, one argues similarly using
[
39
, Theorem 2.1]
instead.

Finally, because
\pi_{v}
is unitary and generic,
b(\lambda,\pi_{v})
is regular in the region
\Re(\lambda)\in\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*,+}}
. by
[
7
, Section 2]
. This proves the last point.
∎

4.4.3
Global Zeta functions

We now go back to the global setting. For any automorphic form
\Phi\in\mathcal{A}(G)
, we may consider the global Whittaker function

W^{\psi_{0}}(g,\Phi)=\int_{[N_{0}]}\Phi(ng)\overline{\psi_{0}}(n)dn,\quad g\in[G].

(4.50)

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we consider the
global Zeta integral

Z(\Phi,s)=\int_{N_{0,H}(\mathbb{A})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})}W^{\psi_{0}}(h,\Phi)\left|\det h\right|^{s}dh.

By
[
10
, Lemma 7.1.1.1]
, this integral is absolutely convergent for
\Re(s)
large enough, and by
[
33
, Corollary 5.3]
, it admits a meromorphic continuation to
\mathbb{C}
.

Let
P
be a standard parabolic subgroup of
G
, let
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathrm{disc}}(M_{P})
. If
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{P,\pi}(G)
, we consider

\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}\mapsto Z_{\pi}(\varphi,\lambda):=Z(E(\varphi,\lambda),0).

This is a meromorphic function in
\lambda
by
[
33
, Corollary 5.4]
and
[
33
, Equation (4.2)]
. Moreover, assume that
\varphi=\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\varphi_{v}
is factorizable and consider the product of global
L
functions
L(\lambda+1/2,\pi_{n}\times\pi_{n+1})
and
b(\lambda,\pi)
which are the analogues of (
4.47
) and (
4.48
). By the unramified computations of
[
16
]
and
[
23
, Theorem 7.1]
, there exists
\mathtt{S}
a finite set of places of
F
such that

Z_{\pi}(\varphi,\lambda)=\frac{L(\lambda+\frac{1}{2},\pi_{n}\times\pi_{n+1})}{b(\lambda,\pi)}\times\prod_{v\in\mathtt{S}}Z_{\pi_{v}}^{\natural}(\varphi_{v},\lambda).

(4.51)

The Zeta function is identically zero as soon as
\pi
is not cuspidal as residual representations are not generic (see
[
47
, Theorem 2.7]
for a proof that their unramified components are not generic).

4.4.4
Enter the Zeta integrals

We now relate the regularized periods
\mathcal{P}
and the Zeta integrals
Z_{\pi}
. We follows the strategy of
[
33
]
. The starting point is a weak form of the fine expansion of the Rankin–Selberg period for pseudo-Eisenstein series which vanish along singular affine hyperplanes. Its proof is nearly identical to that of
[
33
, Lemma 4.9]
and
[
45
, Lemma 9.1.1]
.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
P_{n+1}
be a standard parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}
. Let
\Phi\in\mathcal{P}\mathcal{W}_{P_{n+1}}
be a Paley–Wiener function (see §
2.5
). Let
\varphi_{n}\in\mathcal{A}(\mathrm{GL}_{n})
. Assume that
\Phi(\lambda)
vanishes along the affine hyperplanes of
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
such that

\langle w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\lambda_{n}+\underline{\rho}_{Q},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle=0,

(4.52)

for any
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
,
w\in W(P_{n+1};Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1})
,
\lambda_{n}\in\mathcal{E}_{Q_{n}}(\varphi_{n})
and
\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{Q,H}^{\vee}
(counted with multiplicities). Then for
\kappa\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{*}
sufficiently positive we have

\int_{[H]}\varphi_{n}(h)E(h,F_{\Phi})dh=\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa}\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{n}\otimes E(\Phi(\lambda),\lambda))d\lambda.

Proof.

Let
\kappa\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{*}
be sufficiently positive. By (
2.10
), we have

\int_{[H]}\varphi_{n}(h)E(h,F_{\Phi})dh=\int_{[H]}\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa}\varphi_{n}(h)E(h,\Phi(\lambda),\lambda)d\lambda dh.

(4.53)

By Lemma
4.10
and the computation of the constant term of cuspidal Eisenstein series in (
2.6
), we have for fixed
h
and
\lambda

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(h)E(h,\Phi(\lambda),\lambda)=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\delta\in Q_{H}(F)\backslash H(F)}\tau_{Q}(H_{0,H}(\delta h)_{Q}-T_{Q})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sum_{w\in W(P_{n+1};Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1})}\Lambda^{T,Q}\left((\varphi_{n})_{Q_{n}}\otimes E^{Q_{n+1}}(M(w,\lambda)\Phi(\lambda),w\lambda)\right)(\delta h),

where we write
E^{Q_{n+1}}(M(w,\lambda)\Phi(\lambda),w\lambda)
for
E^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}}(w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std},-1}\cdot,M(w,\lambda)\Phi(\lambda),w\lambda)
. Because these three sums are finite, we see that (
4.53
) is

\int_{[H]}\sum_{Q}\sum_{w}\sum_{\delta}\tau_{Q}(H_{0,H}(\delta h)_{Q}-T_{Q})\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa}\Lambda^{T,Q}\left((\varphi_{n})_{Q_{n}}\otimes E^{Q_{n+1}}(M(w,\lambda)\Phi(\lambda),w\lambda)(\delta h)\right)d\lambda dh.

(4.54)

Fix
Q
and
w
. Write the normalized decomposition

(\varphi_{n})_{Q_{n}}(g)=\sum_{i}q_{Q,i}(H_{Q}(g))\exp(\langle\lambda_{Q,i},H_{Q}(g)\rangle)\varphi_{i}(g),\quad g\in G(\mathbb{A})

as in (
4.28
). Let
\underline{\rho}_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}
be the element defined in §
4.1.6
. Because it belongs to
\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}^{*,+}}
, we see that for every
t>0
it satisfies the following properties:

1.

if
\alpha\in\Delta_{P_{n+1}}
with
w\alpha<0
, then
\langle-tw^{-1}\underline{\rho}_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}},\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\geq 0
;

2.

for every
\alpha\in\Delta_{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1,w}}^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}}
,
\langle-t\underline{\rho}_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}},\alpha^{\vee}\rangle=0
;

3.

for every
\varpi^{\vee}\in\hat{\Delta}_{Q,H}^{\vee}
,
\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\langle-tw_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}\underline{\rho}_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle=\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\langle-t\underline{\rho}_{Q},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle=-\infty
.

By 1 and 2, we can shift the contour of integration of the outermost integral of (
4.54
) to
\Re(\lambda)=\kappa-tw^{-1}\underline{\rho}_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}
without encountering any poles of
M(w,\lambda)
or
E(\cdot,w\lambda)
. Moreover, we stay in their region of absolute convergence by
[
49
]
, so that the integrand is of rapid decay in vertical strips by Theorem
4.9
. By 3, the factorization of the measures (
4.24
), Lemma
4.8
and Theorem
4.9
, for
t
large enough we have

\displaystyle\int_{[H]}\sum_{\delta}\tau_{Q}(H_{0,H}(\delta h)_{Q}-T_{Q})\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa}\Lambda^{T,Q}\left((\varphi_{n})_{Q_{n}}\otimes E^{Q_{n+1}}(M(w,\lambda)\Phi(\lambda),w\lambda)\right)(\delta h)d\lambda dh

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa-tw^{-1}\underline{\rho}_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}}\mathrm{FT}_{Q}(q_{Q,i},T,w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\lambda_{Q,i}+\underline{\rho}_{Q})\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\varphi_{i}\otimes E^{Q_{n+1}}(M(w,\lambda)\Phi(\lambda),w\lambda))d\lambda,

where in the first line the first double integral is absolutely convergent. It follows the first quadruple integral in (
4.54
) is absolutely convergent, and that by shifting back the contour to
\kappa
, which is possible because of the vanishing assumptions on
\Phi
by Lemma
4.8
, it is

\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa}\sum_{Q,w,i}\mathrm{FT}_{Q}(q_{Q,i},T,w_{Q}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\lambda_{i}+\underline{\rho}_{Q})\mathcal{P}^{T,Q}(\varphi_{i}\otimes E^{Q_{n+1}}(M(w,\lambda)\Phi(\lambda),w\lambda))d\lambda.

By (
4.30
) the integrand is
\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{n}\otimes E(\Phi(\lambda),\lambda))
, which concludes.
∎

Proposition 4.20
.

Let
\varphi=\varphi_{n}\otimes\varphi_{n+1}\in\mathcal{A}(G)
. For
s\in\mathbb{C}
, write
\varphi_{s}=\varphi_{n,s}\otimes\varphi_{n+1}
. Then we have the equality of meromorphic functions
\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{s})=Z(\varphi,s)
. In particular, if
P
is a standard parabolic subgroup of
G
and if
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathrm{cusp}}(M_{P})
, we have for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position

\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)=Z_{\pi}(\varphi,\lambda).

Proof.

This is proved as in
[
33
, Theorem 1.1]
. Let us sketch the proof.
[
33
]
, Ichino and Yamana build a regularized period
\mathcal{P}^{IY}
with similar properties as Zydor’s version
\mathcal{P}
. By
[
33
, Lemma 4.9]
, it satisfies

\int_{[H]}\varphi_{n}(h)E(h,F_{\Phi})dh=\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{IY}(\varphi_{n}\otimes E(\Phi(\lambda),\lambda))d\lambda,

under the same vanishing hypotheses on
\Phi
as in Lemma
4.19
. Moreover, it is proved in
[
33
, Lemma 4.7]
that, still with these vanishing requirements, for
s\in\mathbb{C}
with
\Re(s)
large enough and for any
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G(F_{\infty}))
we have

\int_{[H]}\varphi_{n,s}(h)E(h,f*F_{\Phi})dh=\int_{\Re(\lambda)=\kappa}Z\left(\varphi_{n}\otimes E(I_{P}(\lambda,f)\Phi(\lambda),\lambda),s\right)d\lambda.

By repeating the argument of
[
33
, Section 4.10]
, we see that for all
\lambda
sufficiently positive, and therefore that for all regular
\lambda
by analytic continuation we have

\mathcal{P}(\varphi_{n}\otimes E(\Phi(\lambda),\lambda))=\mathcal{P}^{IY}(\varphi_{n}\otimes E(\Phi(\lambda),\lambda))=Z(\varphi_{n}\otimes E(\Phi(\lambda),\lambda),0).

Proposition
4.20
now follows from Franke’s theorem
[
26
]
which states that any automorphic form can be obtained as a linear combination of derivatives of cuspidal Eisenstein series (see
[
33
, Section 4.6]
).
∎

Remark 4.21
.

As a byproduct of the proof of Proposition
4.20
, we obtain the equality
\mathcal{P}(\varphi)=\mathcal{P}^{IY}(\varphi)
for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}(G)^{\mathrm{reg}}
. One may moreover check that the space
\mathcal{A}(G\times G^{\prime})^{*}
of
[
33
, Definition 3.2]
, which is the space of automorphic forms on which
\mathcal{P}^{IY}
is defined, is indeed equal to our
\mathcal{A}(G)^{\mathrm{reg}}
. However, the truncations
\Lambda^{T}
and
\Lambda^{T,IY}
are different. In the case
G=\mathrm{GL}_{1}\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}
, we have
\mathcal{F}_{\RS}=\{G,P_{0},\overline{P}_{0}\}
where
\overline{P}_{0}
is the opposite Borel. Then

\Lambda^{T}\phi(h)=\phi(h)-1_{H_{0,H}(h)\geq T}\cdot\phi_{P_{0}}(h)-1_{H_{0,H}(h)\leq T}\cdot\phi_{\overline{P}_{0}}(h),\quad h\in\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{A}),

(4.55)

while

\Lambda^{T,IY}\phi(h)=\phi(h)-1_{H_{0,H}(h)\geq T}\cdot\phi_{P_{0}}(h)-1_{H_{0,H}(h)\leq-T}\cdot\phi_{\overline{P}_{0}}(h),\quad h\in\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{A}).

4.5
Residues of Rankin–Selberg periods

In this section, we compute some residues of the regularized period
\lambda\mapsto\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)
.

4.5.1
A naive notion of residues

Let
m\geq 1
, let
f
be a meromorphic function on
\mathbb{C}^{m}
. Let
\Lambda
be a non-zero affine linear form on
\mathbb{C}^{m}
. Write
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}
for the affine hyperplane
\{v\in\mathbb{C}^{m}\;|\;\Lambda(v)=0\}
. The map
v\mapsto\Lambda(v)f(v)
is a meromorphic function on
\mathbb{C}^{m}
. Assume that
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}
is not contained in its polar divisor (i.e.
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}
is at most a simple polar divisor of
f
). Then its restriction to
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}
is a meromorphic function on
\mathcal{H}
, and we set

\underset{\Lambda}{\mathrm{Res}}f:=\left(\Lambda f\right)_{|\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}}.

Let
\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{r}
be a family of affine linear forms such that the underlying family of linear forms is linearly disjoint. We consider the iterated residue

\underset{\Lambda_{r}\leftarrow\Lambda_{1}}{\mathrm{Res}}f:=\underset{\Lambda_{r}}{\mathrm{Res}}\ldots\underset{\Lambda_{1}}{\mathrm{Res}}f,

provided each residue is defined in the above sense. This is a meromorphic function on
\mathcal{H}:=\bigcap\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda_{i}}
. Note that the iterated residue a priori depends on the order of the affine linear forms. The following easy lemma gives a condition for when it does not.

Lemma 4.22
.

Assume that there exists a meromorphic function
g
on
\mathbb{C}^{m}
such that

f(\lambda)=\frac{g(\lambda)}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{i}(\lambda)},

and that moreover
\mathcal{H}
is not contained in any of the singularities of
g
. Then the residue
\underset{\Lambda_{r}\leftarrow\Lambda_{1}}{\mathrm{Res}}f
may be taken in any order and we have
\underset{\Lambda_{r}\leftarrow\Lambda_{1}}{\mathrm{Res}}f=g_{|\mathcal{H}}
.

4.5.2
Residues as regularized periods

Let
P
be a standard parabolic subgroup of
G
and
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathrm{cusp}}(M_{P})
. Write the Levi factor
M_{P}=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m_{n}}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{n,i}}\right)\times\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m_{n+1}}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{n+1,j}}\right)
and
\pi=\boxtimes\pi_{n,i}\boxtimes\pi_{n+1,j}
accordingly.

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
1\leq i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}\leq m_{n}
(resp.
1\leq j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}\leq m_{n+1}
) be distinct indices such that
\pi_{n,i_{l}}=\pi_{n+1,j_{l}}^{\vee}
for
1\leq l\leq m
. Set
k=n-\sum_{l}n_{n,i_{l}}
.

1.

For every
l
, let
\Lambda_{l}
be the affine linear form
\Lambda_{l}(\lambda)=\lambda_{n,i_{l}}+\lambda_{n+1,j_{l}}+\frac{1}{2}
. Let
Q_{n+1}^{\mathrm{std}}
be the standard parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}
of standard Levi factor
\left(\prod_{l=1}^{m}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{n,i_{l}}}\right)\times\mathrm{GL}_{k+1}
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
be the element corresponding to
(Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1},m+1)
under the bijection of Corollary
4.2
. Let
w=(w_{n},w_{n+1})\in W(P;Q^{\mathrm{std}})
be the only element such that
w_{n}(i_{l})=w_{n+1}(j_{l})=l
for
1\leq l\leq m
. Then for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{P,\pi}(G)
we have

\underset{\Lambda_{m}\leftarrow\Lambda_{1}}{\mathrm{Res}}\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)=(-1)^{m}\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w).

(4.56)

2.

For every
l
, let
\Lambda_{l}
be the affine linear form
\Lambda_{l}(\lambda)=\lambda_{n,i_{l}}+\lambda_{n+1,j_{l}}-\frac{1}{2}
. Let
Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1}
be the standard parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}
with standard Levi factor
\mathrm{GL}_{k+1}\times\left(\prod_{l=m}^{1}\mathrm{GL}_{n_{n,i_{l}}}\right)
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\RS}
be the element corresponding to
(Q^{\mathrm{std}}_{n+1},1)
under the bijection of Corollary
4.2
. Let
w=(w_{n},w_{n+1})\in W(P;Q^{\mathrm{std}})
be the only element such that
w_{n}(i_{l})=m_{n}-l+1
and
w_{n+1}(j_{l})=m_{n+1}-l+1
for
1\leq l\leq m
. Then for every
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{P,\pi}(G)
we have

\underset{\Lambda_{m}\leftarrow\Lambda_{1}}{\mathrm{Res}}\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)=\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda,w).

(4.57)

Proof.

We begin with the first case (
4.56
) under the assumption that
m=1
. By the functional equation of Eisenstein series
[
9
, Theorem 2.3]
, we have for
\lambda
in general position

\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)=\mathcal{P}(M(w,\lambda)\varphi,w\lambda).

Note that
M(w,\lambda)\varphi
is regular for
\lambda
in general position in the affine hyperplane
\lambda_{n,i}+\lambda_{{n+1},j}+\frac{1}{2}=0
. It follows from Corollary
4.17
that it is enough to prove (
4.56
) for
i=j=1
and
w=1
.

We now assume that
i=j=1
and
w=1
. We set
\Lambda(\lambda)=\lambda_{n,1}+\lambda_{n+1,1}+\frac{1}{2}
. By the first statement of Proposition
4.16
, we have for any
T
sufficiently positive

\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)=\sum_{R}\varepsilon_{R}\sum_{w\in W(P;R^{\mathrm{std}})}\mathcal{P}^{T,R}(\varphi,\lambda,w)\cdot\frac{\exp(\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R},T_{R}\rangle)}{\hat{\theta}_{R}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R})}.

(4.58)

By the description of
\hat{\theta}_{R}
in §
4.1.4
and §
4.2
, we see that
\Lambda
divides
\hat{\theta}_{R}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R})
if and only if
\mathbf{M}_{R,+}
is of the form
(\mathrm{GL}_{n_{n,1}}\times\ldots)
and
w_{n}(1)=1
,
w_{n+1}(1)=1
. In that case, denote by
\hat{\theta}_{R}^{\natural}
the quotient of
\hat{\theta}_{R}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R})
by
\Lambda
. By
[
9
, Theorem 2.3]
, the generalized Eisenstein series
E^{R^{\mathrm{std}}}(M(w,\lambda)\varphi,w\lambda)
appearing in (
4.58
) are regular along our affine hyperplane. By Theorem
4.9
and the continuity of Eisenstein series from
[
46
, Theorem 2.2]
, so are their truncated periods so that

\underset{\Lambda}{\mathrm{Res}}\;\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)=\sum_{(R,w)}\varepsilon_{R}\mathcal{P}^{T,R}(\varphi,\lambda,w)\cdot\frac{\exp(\langle w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R},T_{R}\rangle)}{\hat{\theta}^{\natural}_{R}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R})},

(4.59)

where
(R,w)
ranges in the couples we just described. But these are exactly the pairs with
R\subset Q
a Rankin–Selberg parabolic subgroup of
G
and
w\in W^{Q^{\mathrm{std}}}(Q^{\mathrm{std}};R^{\mathrm{std}})
. Moreover, because of the volume computation of (
4.17
),
\hat{\theta}^{\natural}_{R}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R})
is equal to the restriction to
\Lambda^{-1}(\{0\})
of
\hat{\theta}^{Q}_{R}(w_{R}^{\mathrm{std}}w\lambda+\underline{\rho}_{R})
. Because
\varepsilon_{R}^{Q}=-\varepsilon_{R}
, it follows from Proposition
4.16
that

\underset{\Lambda}{\mathrm{Res}}\;\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)=-\mathcal{P}^{Q}(\varphi,\lambda).

(4.60)

To prove (
4.56
) if
m\geq 1
, it remains to do induction on
k
thanks to (
4.60
) and to use parabolic descent by Lemma
4.12
.

The second case is exactly the same, the only difference being a minus sign which appears because of the signs in
4.14
.
∎

5
Residues of global Zeta integrals

Let
\Pi
be an automorphic representation of Arthur type of
G
, realized as a quotient of
\mathcal{A}_{P_{\pi},\pi,-\nu_{\pi}}(G)
as in §
3.2.2
. In this section, we build a
H(\mathbb{A})
-invariant linear form
\mathcal{P}_{\pi}
on
\mathcal{A}_{P_{\pi},\pi,-\nu_{\pi}}(G)
, and show that it factors through
\Pi
if
\Pi
is in general position (see Theorem
5.2
). Because it is a residue of a global Zeta integral, it admits an Euler product decomposition. We also show in §
5.4
that
\mathcal{P}_{\pi}
is a regularized period along a degeneracy of
[H]
.

5.1
Periods for relevant inducing pairs

Because
\mathcal{P}_{\pi}
is defined on an induction, we prefer to keep track of the choice of the inducing data for
\Pi
. We translate the relevance condition of §
1.1.1
for Arthur parameters in this language.

5.1.1
The relevance condition

Let
\Pi_{H}
be the set of
relevant inducing pairs
. This is the set of pairs
(P,\pi)
where
P
is a standard parabolic subgroup of
G
whose standard Levi factor
M_{P}=M_{P,n}\times M_{P,n+1}
admits a decomposition

\displaystyle M_{P,n}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\mathrm{GL}_{d(1,i)r(1,i)}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\mathrm{GL}_{(d(2,i)-1)r(2,i)},

(5.1)

\displaystyle M_{P,{n+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\mathrm{GL}_{(d(1,i)-1)r(1,i)}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\mathrm{GL}_{d(2,i)r(2,i)};

(5.2)

and
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathrm{disc}}(M_{P})
is a discrete automorphic representation (with trivial central character on
A_{P}^{\infty}
) and such that, with respect to (
5.1
) and (
5.2
),
\pi=\pi_{n}\boxtimes\pi_{n+1}
decomposes as

\pi_{n}=\boxtimes_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\pi_{1,i}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\pi_{2,i}^{-,\vee},\quad\pi_{n+1}=\boxtimes_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\pi_{1,i}^{-,\vee}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\pi_{2,i},

(5.3)

where we set
\mathrm{Speh}(\sigma,d)^{-}=\mathrm{Speh}(\sigma,d-1)
, and we have
\pi_{1,i}=\mathrm{Speh}(\sigma_{1,i},d(1,i))
,
\pi_{2,i}=\mathrm{Speh}(\sigma_{2,i},d(2,i))
for some representations
\sigma_{1,i}\in\Pi_{\mathrm{cusp}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)})
and
\sigma_{2,i}\in\Pi_{\mathrm{cusp}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)})
. By convention
\mathrm{Speh}(\sigma,0)
is the trivial representation of the trivial group.

Let
(P,\pi)\in\Pi_{H}
. With the choices of coordinates made in §
3.1.1
,

\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}
is realized as a subspace

\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}\subset\left(\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}\right)\times\left(\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}\right).

(5.4)

A similar decomposition holds for
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. If
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}
, we write

\lambda=\left((\lambda(1)_{n},\lambda(2)_{n}),(\lambda(1)_{n+1},\lambda(2)_{n+1})\right)

(5.5)

according to this decomposition. Note that
\lambda(2)_{n,i}=0
if
d(2,i)=1
, and
\lambda(1)_{n+1,i}=0
if
d(1,i)=1
.

We define the anti-diagonal subspace
\mathfrak{a}_{\pi}^{*}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}
to be

\mathfrak{a}_{\pi}^{*}=\left\{\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda(1)_{n,i}=-\lambda(1)_{n+1,i},&1\leq i\leq m_{1},\;\text{if }d(1,i)\neq 1,\\
\lambda(2)_{n,i}=-\lambda(2)_{n+1,i},&1\leq i\leq m_{2},\;\text{if }d(2,i)\neq 1,\end{array}\right\}.

(5.6)

We have an isomorphism

\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{\pi}^{*}\mapsto\left(\lambda(1),\lambda(2)\right):=\left(\lambda(1)_{n},\lambda(2)_{n+1}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}.

(5.7)

We also have an anti-diagonal subspace
\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}\subset\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
defined by the same equations. Note that
i\mathfrak{a}_{\pi}^{*}
is exactly the subspace of
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
such that
(P,\pi_{\lambda})\in\Pi_{H}
if we lift the requirement that the central character is trivial on
A_{P}^{\infty}
, and ask that it is unitary instead.

5.1.2
Singular affine linear planes

Let
(P,\pi)\in\Pi_{H}
be a relevant inducing pair. Let
\sigma_{\pi}
and
\nu_{\pi}
be respectively the cuspidal automorphic representation of
M_{P_{\pi}}
and the element of
\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi}}^{*}
such that
\mathcal{A}_{P,\pi}(G)
is obtained by taking residues of Eisenstein series on the induction
\mathcal{A}_{P_{\pi},\sigma_{\pi},-\nu_{\pi}}(G)
(see §
3.2.2
). For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position, we have the global Zeta function
Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\cdot,\lambda)
from §
4.4.3
. We can identify
\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}-\nu_{\pi}
with a subspace of
\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. It is contained in a finite union of singularities of
Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\cdot,\lambda)
which are all affine hyperplanes. We now describe the corresponding affine linear forms.

By assumption, we can write
M_{P_{\pi}}
the standard Levi factor of
P_{\pi}
as follows:

\displaystyle M_{P_{\pi},n}=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)})^{d(1,i)}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)})^{d(2,i)-1},

(5.8)

\displaystyle M_{P_{\pi},n+1}=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)})^{d(1,i)-1}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)})^{d(2,i)}.

(5.9)

This yields an identification

\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi},\mathbb{C}}^{*}=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\mathbb{C}^{d(1,i)}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\mathbb{C}^{d(2,i)-1}\right)\times\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\mathbb{C}^{d(1,i)-1}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\mathbb{C}^{d(2,i)}\right).

We will from now on write the coordinates of any
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
with respect to these identifications. More precisely, if
\lambda=(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{n+1})\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
we write
\lambda_{n}
as

(\lambda(1,1)_{n},\ldots,\lambda(1,m_{1})_{n},\lambda(2,1)_{n},\ldots,\lambda(2,m_{2})_{n})

(5.10)

where for example
\lambda(1,1)_{n}\in\mathbb{C}^{d(1,1)}
has coordinates
\lambda(1,1)_{n,1},\ldots,\lambda(1,1)_{n,d(1,1)}
. The same applies for
\lambda_{n+1}
.

We now build a set
L_{+}
of affine linear forms on
\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
. They are defined as

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Lambda_{+}(1,i,j)(\lambda)=-(\lambda(1,i)_{n,d(1,i)-j+1}+\lambda(1,i)_{n+1,j}+1/2),&\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1\leq i\leq m_{1},\\
1\leq j\leq d(1,i)-1,\end{array}\right.\\
\Lambda_{+}(2,i,j)(\lambda)=-(\lambda(2,i)_{n,j}+\lambda(2,i)_{n+1,d({2,i})-j+1}+1/2),&\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1\leq i\leq m_{2},\\
1\leq j\leq d(2,i)-1.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

(5.11)

Set
\mathcal{H}_{+}=\cap_{\Lambda_{+}\in L_{+}}\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda_{+}}
. We also have the set
L_{-}
of linear forms defined by the following equations.

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Lambda_{-}(1,i,j)(\lambda)=\lambda(1,i)_{n,d(1,i)-j}+\lambda(1,i)_{n+1,j}-1/2,&\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1\leq i\leq m_{1},\\
1\leq j\leq d(1,i)-1,\end{array}\right.\\
\Lambda_{-}(2,i,j)(\lambda)=\lambda(2,i)_{n,j}+\lambda(2,i)_{n+1,d(2,i)-j}-1/2,&\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1\leq i\leq m_{2},\\
1\leq j\leq d(2,i)-1.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

(5.12)

Set
\mathcal{H}_{-}=\cap_{\Lambda_{-}\in L_{-}}\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda_{-}}
. If we restrict to
\lambda\in\mathcal{H}_{+}
, we have (for
i
and
j
in the suitable range)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Lambda_{-}(1,i,j)(\lambda)=\lambda(1,i)_{n,d(1,i)-j}-\lambda(1,i)_{n,d(1,i)-j+1}-1=\lambda(1,i)_{n+1,j}-\lambda(1,i)_{n+1,j+1}-1,\\
\Lambda_{-}(2,i,j)(\lambda)=\lambda(2,i)_{n,j}-\lambda(2,i)_{n,j+1}-1=\lambda(2,i)_{n+1,d(2,i)-j}-\lambda(2,i)_{n+1,d(2,i)-j+1}-1.\end{array}\right.

(5.13)

It follows that

\mathcal{H}_{+}\cap\mathcal{H}_{-}=\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}-\nu_{\pi}.

(5.14)

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{A}_{P_{\pi},\sigma_{\pi},-\nu_{\pi}}(G)
. The residue
\underset{L_{+}\cup L_{-}}{\mathrm{Res}}\;Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\phi,\lambda)
is a well-defined meromorphic function on
\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}-\nu_{\pi}
and can be computed with respect to any order on the set
L_{+}\cup L_{-}
.

Proof.

We can assume that
\phi=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\phi_{v}
is factorizable. By (
4.51
), we have the Euler product expansion for
\mathtt{S}
large enough

Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\phi,\lambda)=\frac{L(\lambda+1/2,\sigma_{\pi,n}\times\sigma_{\pi,n+1})}{b(\lambda,\sigma_{\pi})}\prod_{v\in\mathtt{S}}Z^{\sharp}_{\sigma_{\pi},v}(\phi_{v},\lambda).

Because
-\nu_{\pi}\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\pi}}^{P,*,+}
, we see using the description of
\mathfrak{a}_{\pi}^{*}
in (
5.6
), that for every
\alpha\in\Sigma_{P_{\pi}}
the map
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}-\nu_{\pi}\mapsto\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle
is either non-constant, either constant equal to a positive integer. By Lemma
4.18
, we conclude that
\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}-\nu_{\pi}
is not contained in any of the singularities of the product of the local terms, which all come from
b(\lambda,\sigma_{\pi,v})
. By the properties of Rankin–Selberg
L
-functions, the affine linear forms in
L_{+}\cup L_{-}
direct all the singularities of the quotient of global
L
-functions containing
\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}-\nu_{\pi}
, which are all affine hyperplanes with multiplicity one coming from
L(\lambda+1/2,\sigma_{\pi,n}\times\sigma_{\pi,n+1})
. Therefore, Lemma
4.22
concludes.
∎

5.1.3
Residues of Zeta functions and main result

Let
(P,\pi)\in\Pi_{H}
be a relevant inducing pair. We denote by
\mathrm{Res}\;Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\cdot,\lambda)
the iterated residue obtained in Lemma
5.1
. For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
we set, with the coordinates of (
5.7
),

\mathcal{L}(\lambda,\pi)=\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{i,j}L\left(\lambda(1)_{i}+\lambda(2)_{j}+\frac{d(1,i)-d(2,j)+1}{2},\sigma_{1,i}\times\sigma_{2,j}\right)}{\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{2}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq m_{k}}L\left(\lambda(k)_{i}-\lambda(k)_{j}+\frac{d(k,i)+d(k,j)}{2},\sigma_{k,i}\times\sigma_{k,j}^{\vee}\right)}.

Theorem 5.2
.

The following properties hold.

1.

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position,
\mathrm{Res}\;Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\cdot,\lambda-\nu_{\pi})
factors through
\mathcal{A}_{P_{\pi},\sigma_{\pi},\lambda-\nu_{\pi}}(G)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{P,\pi,\lambda}(G)
and yields a
H(\mathbb{A})
-invariant linear form
\mathcal{P}_{\pi}(\cdot,\lambda)
on this induction.

2.

There exists a regular never vanishing function
\varepsilon
(which is a product of epsilon factors and of special values of
L
-functions) such that for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}_{\pi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
in general position and
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{P,\pi}(G)
with
\varphi=E^{P,*}(\phi,-\nu_{\pi})
and
\phi=\otimes_{v}\phi_{v}
we have

\mathcal{P}_{\pi}(\varphi,\lambda)=\varepsilon(\lambda)\mathcal{L}(\lambda,\pi)\prod_{v\in\mathtt{S}}Z_{\sigma_{\pi,v}}^{\natural}(\phi_{v},\lambda-\nu_{\pi}),

where
\mathtt{S}
is any sufficiently large finite set of places of
F
.

Remark 5.3
.

The possible singularities of
\mathcal{P}_{\pi}(\varphi,\lambda)
come either from
\mathcal{L}(\lambda,\pi)
, either from the
Z_{\sigma_{\pi},v}^{\natural}
. It turns out that the latter are regular for
\lambda\in i\mathfrak{a}_{\pi}^{*}
. This will be proved in Theorem
6.1
.

5.2
Computation of the residues

We now compute
\mathrm{Res}\;Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\cdot,\lambda-\nu_{\pi})
by varying the order of the linear forms in
L_{+}\cup L_{-}
. Because we know that
Z_{\sigma_{\pi}}(\cdot,\lambda-\nu_{\pi})
is
\mathcal{P}(\cdot,\lambda)
(Proposition
4.20
), we can use Proposition
4.23
.

5.2.1
Residues along
L_{+}

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{A}_{P_{\pi},\sigma_{\pi}}(G)
. By Lemma
5.1
, we have the meromorphic function
\underset{L_{+}}{\mathrm{Res}}\;\mathcal{P}(\phi,\lambda)
on
\mathcal{H}_{+}
. We compute it using Proposition
4.23
by varying the order in
L_{+}
.

We start by taking the order

\left(\xleftarrow[i=1]{m_{2}}\xleftarrow[j=1]{d(2,i)-1}\Lambda_{+}(2,i,j)\right)\leftarrow\left(\xleftarrow[i=1]{m_{1}}\xleftarrow[j=d(1,i)-1]{1}\Lambda_{+}(1,i,j)\right)

(5.15)

where for example the notation
\xleftarrow[i=1]{m_{1}}\xleftarrow[j=d(1,i)-1]{1}\Lambda_{+}(1,i,j)
means

\Lambda_{+}(1,m_{1},1)\leftarrow\ldots\leftarrow\Lambda_{+}(1,2,d(1,2)-1)\leftarrow\Lambda_{+}(1,1,1)\leftarrow\ldots\leftarrow\Lambda_{+}(1,1,d(1,1)-1)

By Proposition
4.23
, we have an explicit description of
\underset{L_{+}}{\mathrm{Res}}\;\mathcal{P}(\varphi,\lambda)
. Set

k=\sum_{i=1}^{m_{1}}r(1,i)=\sum_{i=1}^{m_{2}}r(2,i)-1.

(5.16)

Let
P_{\res,n+1}^{\mathrm{std}}
be the standard parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}
with standard Levi factor

M_{P_{\res}^{\mathrm{std}},n+1}=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)})^{d(1,i)-1}\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)})^{d(2,i)-1}\times\mathrm{GL}_{k+1}.

(5.17)

To ease notation, set

d(1,\leq i)=\sum_{j=1}^{i}(d(1,j)-1),\quad d(2,\leq i)=\sum_{j=1}^{i}(d(2,j)-1),\quad D=d(1,\leq m_{1})+d(2,\leq m_{2}).

(5.18)

Let
P_{\res}
be the Rankin–Selberg parabolic subgroup of
G
associated to
(P^{\mathrm{std}}_{\res,n+1},D+1)
by the bijection of Corollary
4.2
. Note that it is standard. We simply write
\mathbf{M}_{\res}^{2}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\res}
for
\mathbf{M}_{P_{\res}}^{2}
and
\mathcal{M}_{P_{\res}}
respectively. We now describe the element
w
appearing in (
4.56
). It belongs to
W(P_{\pi};P_{\res})
. We first build an element
w_{1}=(w_{1,n},w_{1,n+1})\in W(P_{\pi})
. Let
m_{\pi,n}
(resp.
m_{\pi,n+1}
) be the number of blocks of
M_{P_{\pi},n}
(resp.
M_{P_{\pi},n+1}
) so that we identify
w_{1,n}
with a permutation in
\mathfrak{S}(m_{\pi,n})
(resp.
w_{1,n+1}
with a permutation in
\mathfrak{S}(m_{\pi,n+1})
). Then
w_{1}
is a product of cycles. More precisely, for every
1\leq i\leq m_{1}
let
w_{n,i}\in\mathfrak{S}(m_{\pi,n})
be the cycle

w_{n,i}=\left(d(1,\leq i-1)+1\quad D+m_{1}\quad D+m_{1}-1\quad\ldots\quad d(1,\leq i-1)+2\right)

and let for
1\leq i\leq m_{2}
let
w_{n+1,i}\in\mathfrak{S}(m_{\pi,n+1})
be the cycle following the same pattern

w_{n+1,i}=\left(d(1,\leq m_{1})+d(2,\leq i)+1\quad D+m_{2}\quad\ldots\quad d(1,\leq m_{1})+d(2,\leq i)+2\right)

Then we set

w_{1,n}=w_{n,m_{1}}\ldots w_{n,1},\quad w_{1,n+1}=w_{n+1,m_{2}}\ldots w_{n+1,1},\quad w_{1}=(w_{1,n},w_{1,n+1}).

(5.19)

More concretely,
w_{1,n}
sends
M_{P_{\pi},n}
to

\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)})^{d(1,i)-1}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)})^{d(2,i)-1}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)},

(5.20)

by sending the first
\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)}
in each product
(\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)})^{d(1,i)}
to the bottom-right corner. On the other hand,
w_{1,n+1}
sends
M_{P_{\pi},n+1}
to

\prod_{i=1}^{m_{1}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(1,i)})^{d(1,i)-1}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}(\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)})^{d(2,i)-1}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)},

(5.21)

this time by sending the last block of each
(\mathrm{GL}_{r(2,i)})^{d(2,i)}
to the corner. By elementary computations, the element
w
which appears in (
4.56
) is
w_{1}w_{\pi,n+1}^{*}
, where
w_{\pi,n+1}^{*}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n+1}
is the element defined in §
3.2.2
such that
\mathcal{A}_{P_{n+1},\pi_{n+1}}(\mathrm{GL}_{n+1})
is the image of
M^{*}(w_{\pi,n+1},-\nu_{\pi,n+1})
. By Proposition
4.23
, we have for
\lambda\in\mathcal{H}_{+}
in general position

\underset{L_{+}}{\mathrm{Res}}\;\mathcal{P}(\phi,\lambda)=\mathcal{P}^{P_{\res}}(\phi,\lambda,w_{1}w_{\pi,n+1}^{*}).

(5.22)

We now compute
\underset{L_{+}}{\mathrm{Res}}\;\mathcal{P}(\phi,\lambda)
by choosing a different order on
L_{+}
. Consider

\left(\xleftarrow[i=1]{m_{2}}\xleftarrow[j=d(2,i)-1]{1}\Lambda_{+}(2,i,j)\right)\leftarrow\left(\xleftarrow[i=1]{m_{1}}\xleftarrow[j=1]{d(1,i)-1}\Lambda_{+}(1,i,j)\right)

(5.23)

For every
1\leq i\leq m_{1}
, let
w_{n,i}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}(m_{\pi,n})
be the cycle

w_{n,i}^{\prime}=\left(d(1,\leq i)+1\quad D+m_{1}\quad D+m_{1}-1\quad\ldots\quad d(1,\leq i)+2\right)

and for
1\leq i\leq m_{2}
, set

w_{n+1,i}^{\prime}=\left(d(1,\leq m_{1})+d(2,\leq i-1)+1\quad D+m_{2}\quad\ldots\quad d(1,\leq m_{1})+d(2,\leq i-1)+2\right).

Write

w_{2,n}=w_{n,m_{1}}^{\prime}\ldots w_{n,1}^{\prime},\quad w_{2,n+1}=w_{n+1,m_{2}}^{\prime}\ldots w_{n+1,1}^{\prime},\quad w_{2}=(w_{2,n},w_{2,n+1}).

(5.24)

The action of
w_{2}
can be described as in (
5.20
) and (
5.21
). In particular,
w_{1}w_{\pi,n+1}^{*}.P_{\pi}=w_{2}w_{\pi,n}^{*}.P_{\pi}
. Another application of Proposition
4.23
gives for
\lambda\in\mathcal{H}_{+}
in general position
